5
Roots in one vortex
In this section, we deal with X-Y paths where at least one end is in a fixed vortex. The
main result roughly states that, assuming that there do not exist many pairwise far apart
X-Y paths in G, there exists a centered set hitting all X-Y paths with both ends in a fixed
vortex, and if we cannot hit all X-Y paths with exactly one end in this vortex, then there
is certain structure that will be helpful to construct X-Y paths in the whole graph in the
future. We will prove this main result in Section 5.3. For preparation, we introduce simple
notions that relate paths in G and paths in the skeleton of an arrangement of its segregation
in Section 5.1 and state a known result in the literature in Section 5.2.
5.1
Natural projections and liftings
Let S be a segregation of a graph G effective with respect to a (κ, ρ)-witness (S1, S2) for
some κ, ρ ∈N0. Let α be a proper arrangement of S in a surface Σ. Let G′ be the skeleton
of α with respect to (S1, S2). For a path P in G, the natural projection of P in G′ is the
subgraph P ′ of G′ obtained from P −S
(S,Ω)∈S2 E(S) by, for each (S, Ω) ∈S1, replacing each
52


## Page 53


maximal subpath Q of P ∩S internally disjoint from Ωby G′[V (Q)∩Ω]∩∂α(S, Ω); note that
P ′ is a union of disjoint paths. On the other hand, given a path Q′ in G′, a lifting of Q′ in
G is a path in G −S
(S,Ω)∈S2 E(S) obtained from Q′ by replacing each edge e of Q′ by a path
in Se between the ends of e, where (Se, Ωe) is the member of S1 such that e ⊆∂α(Se, Ωe).
Since S is effective with respect to (S1, S2), every path in G′ has a lifting in G.
5.2
Nested neighborhoods
We say that (S, Ω, Ω0) is a neighborhood if S is a graph and Ω, Ω0 are cyclic permutations
with Ω, Ω0 ⊆V (S). A neighborhood (S, Ω, Ω0) is rural if S has a drawing Γ in the plane
and there are disks ∆0 ⊆∆such that
• Γ uses no point outside ∆and no point in the interior of ∆0,
• Ωare the vertices in Γ ∩∂∆, and Ω0 are the vertices in Γ ∩∆0, and
• the cyclic permutations of Ωand Ω0 coincide with the natural cyclic orders on ∂∆and
∂∆0, respectively.
In this case, we say that (Γ, ∆, ∆0) is a presentation of (S, Ω, Ω0). For a fixed presentation
(Γ, ∆, ∆0) of a neighborhood (S, Ω, Ω0) and an integer s ≥0, an s-nest for (Γ, ∆, ∆0) is a
sequence (C1, C2, ..., Cs) of pairwise disjoint cycles of S such that ∆0 ⊆∆1 ⊆... ⊆∆s ⊆∆
and ∆0 ∩∂∆1 = ∅, where ∆i is the closed disk in the plane bounded by Ci in the drawing
Γ for each i ∈[s].
If (S, Ω, Ω0) is a neighborhood and (S0, Ω0) is a society, then the composition of (S0, Ω0)
with (S, Ω, Ω0) is the society (S ∪S0, Ω). A society (S, Ω) is s-nested if it is the composition
of a society with a rural neighborhood that has an s-nest for some its presentation.
A subgraph F ⊆S for a rural neighborhood (S, Ω, Ω0) with presentation (Γ, ∆, ∆0) is
perpendicular to an s-nest (C1, C2, ..., Cs) for (Γ, ∆, ∆0) if every component P of F is a path
between Ωand Ω0 such that P ∩Ci is a path for each i ∈[s].
We will use a special case of [32, Lemma 8.2].
Lemma 5.1 ([32, Lemma 8.2]). For any k, s ∈N, there exists s′ ∈N with s′ ≥s such that

neighborhood (S′, Ω, Ω0) that has an s′-nest (C1, C2, ..., Cs′) for some presentation (Γ, ∆, ∆0),
and F is a union of k disjoint paths in S each having one end in Ωand one end in V (S0),

5.3
Main lemma
Let G be a graph, and let L be an induced subgraph. Let X and Y be subsets of V (L).
Let ℓbe a nonnegative integer. An (ℓ, X, Y )-path in (L, G) is a path in L between a vertex
x in X and a vertex y in Y such that distG(x, y) ≥ℓ.
An interesting tuple is a tuple (G, L, Z, ξ, η, X, Y, F, ℓ, k, r, TL, θ′, θ, S, κ, ρ, S1, S2, Σ, g, ϕ, α)
such that the following hold:
• G is a graph, and L is an induced subgraph of G.
• Z ⊆V (G) is a (ξ, η)-centered set in G such that NG(V (L)) ⊆Z.
• X and Y are subsets of V (L).
• F is the set of all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (L, G).
• No k members of F have pairwise distance in G at least r.
• TL is the (G, F, r, θ′, Z)-tangle in L, and θ′ ≥θ.
• S is a TL-central segregation of L with a (κ, ρ)-witness (S1, S2).
• Σ is a surface of Euler genus at most g.
• ϕ : Z →R is a nondecreasing function.
• α is an (8, r, S1, S2)-protected (Σ, θ, ϕ, TL)-arrangement of S in Σ with respect to
(S1, S2).
Lemma 5.2. For any k, r, ρ, s, k′ ∈N, there exist λ∗, ϕ∗∈N such that for every κ ∈N0,
there exists θ ∈N such that for any ξ, η ∈N and ℓ, g ∈N0, there exist ξ∗, η∗∈N with
η∗= 4η + 6r + ℓsuch that the following hold.
If (G, L, Z, ξ, η, X, Y, F, ℓ, k, r, TL, θ′, θ, S, κ, ρ, S1, S2, Σ, g, ϕ∗, α) is an interesting tuple,
then for every (S, Ω) ∈S2, the following statements hold.

Roots in all vortices
Recall that in Section 5, we show that for each vortex (S, Ω), there exists ΞS ∈{X, Y }
such that either there exists a hitting set for all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths having an end in V (S) ∩ΞS,
or there exists a certain structure that will be useful for constructing (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in the
future. Note that ΞS depends on the vortex (S, Ω). We will strengthen this result to make
ΞS independent of (S, Ω) in this section.
We need the following notions about homotopy.
Let Σ be a surface and X a subset of Σ. Let ∆1 and ∆2 be open disks in Σ with disjoint
closure. A ∆1-∆2-line in Σ −X is a line in Σ −(X ∪∆1 ∪∆2) with one endpoint in ∆1
and one endpoint in ∆2 internally disjoint from ∆1 ∪∆2. We say that two disjoint ∆1-∆2
61


## Page 62


lines L1 and L2 in Σ −X are homotopic in Σ −X with respect to ∆1 and ∆2 if the union of
L1 ∪L2 and a subset of ∂∆1 ∪∂∆2 bounds a disk in Σ −(X ∪∆1 ∪∆2).
We will use the following restatement [27, Lemma 7.1.4], which is a simple corollary of
[29, Theorem 3.4].
Lemma 6.1 ([27, Lemma 7.1.4]). For any g, κ ∈N0 with κ ≥2, there exists ρ ∈N such that
the following holds. Let Σ be a connected surface of Euler genus at most g. Let ∆1, ∆2, ..., ∆κ
be open disks in Σ with disjoint closure. If S is a set of ∆1-∆2-lines in Σ −Sκ
i=1 ∆i in-
ternally disjoint from Sκ
i=1 ∆i such that members of S are pairwise disjoint and pairwise
non-homotopic in Σ −Sκ
i=1 ∆i with respect to ∆1 and ∆2, then |S| ≤ρ.
Now we are ready to prove the main result of this section.

7
Roots not in vortices
In this section we deal with the (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (L, G) having at least one end not
contained in a vortex, which is the last key ingredient of our proof.
Recall that in the
previous lemma, we show that if a hitting cannot be constructed for (ℓ, X, Y )-paths rooted
at vortices, then there is certain nice structure around the vortices that will be helpful for
constructing other (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in the future. We show that similar nice structures also
66


## Page 67


exist at somewhere far away from all vortices in Section 7.1. Then we prove the main result
of this section in Section 7.2.
7.1
Freeness
Let G be a graph, and let T be a tangle in G. We say that a subset X of V (G) is free
with respect to T if there exists no (A, B) ∈T of order less than |X| such that X ⊆V (A).
Lemma 7.1. For any s, k′, λ, t ∈N, there exists λ∗∈N with λ∗≥λ such that for any

a connected surface Σ. Let T be a respectful tangle in Γ of order at least θ. Let Λ1, Λ2, ..., Λk
be λ-zones in Γ. Then there exist distinct vertices v1, v2, ..., vt ∈V (Γ) such that the following
hold.

8
Minor-closed families
In this section, we prove the weak coarse Menger property for minor-closed families. We
will prove the case when all graphs in the class are finite in Section 8.1. Then we use it
to prove the case for locally finite graphs in Section 8.2. In Section 8.3, we give a simpler
proof for apex-minor free (finite) graphs with a better bound on the radius of the balls in
the hitting set.
8.1
Finite graphs
Lemma 8.1. For any non-planar graph H and k, r, ℓ∈N, there exists θ ∈N such that for
any ξ, η ∈N, there exist ξ∗, η∗∈N with η∗= 4η + (4g + 14)r + ℓ, where g is the largest
nonnegative integer such that H cannot be drawn in some surface of Euler genus g, such that
the following holds.
Let G be a graph, and let L be an induced subgraph of G. Let Z ⊆V (G) be a (ξ, η)-
centered set in G such that NG(V (L)) ⊆Z. Let X and Y be subsets of V (L). Let F be the
set of all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (L, G). Assume that no k members of F have pairwise distance
in G at least r. Let TL be the (G, F, r, θ′, Z)-tangle in L for some integer θ′ ≥θ. Let TG be
a tangle of order at least θ induced by TL. If TG does not control an H-minor, then there
exists Z∗⊆V (G) such that Z∗is (ξ∗, η∗)-centered in G and intersects all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in
(L, G).
Proof. Let H be a non-planar graph, and let k, r, ℓ∈N.

Now we prove Theorem 2.1, which is the main result for finite graphs in a minor-closed
family in this paper. Recall that the planar H case of Theorem 2.1 follows from Lemma
3.3. The non-planar H case follows from the following theorem, which is a cleaner version
of Lemma 8.1.
Theorem 8.2. For any non-planar graph H, any k, r ∈N and ℓ, ξ, η ∈N0, there exist
ξ∗, η∗∈N with η∗= 4η + (4g + 22)r + ℓ, where g is the largest nonnegative integer such
that H cannot be drawn in some surface of Euler genus g, such that the following holds. Let
G be an H-minor free graph, and let L be an induced subgraph of G. Let Z ⊆V (G) be a
(ξ, η)-centered set in G such that NG(V (L)) ⊆Z. Let X and Y be subsets of V (L). If no k
(ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (L, G) have pairwise distance in G at least r, then there exists Z∗⊆V (G)
such that Z∗is (ξ∗, η∗)-centered in G intersecting all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (L, G).
Proof. Let H be a non-planar graph, and let k, r ∈N and ℓ, ξ, η ∈N0. Let g be the

8.2
Locally finite infinite graphs
In this section, we will prove a version of Theorem 8.2 for locally finite infinite graphs by
using a compactness argument. We use a result of Gottschalk [22] about choice functions.
A choice function of a family (Xα)α∈I of sets is a function c such that c(α) ∈Xα for every
α ∈I.
Theorem 8.3 ([22]). Let I be a set. Let (Xα)α∈I be a family of finite sets, let A be the class
of all finite subsets of I. For every A ∈A, let cA be a choice function of (Xα)α∈A. Then
there exists a choice function c of (Xα)α∈I such that for every A ∈A, there exists B ∈A
with B ⊇A such that c(α) = cB(α) for every α ∈A.
Theorem 8.4. For any (finite) graph H, any k, r ∈N and ℓ∈N0, there exist ξ∗, η∗∈N
and c ∈R>0 with η∗≤c(r + ℓ) such that the following holds. Let G be an H-minor free
finite or locally finite infinite graph, and let X and Y be subsets of V (G). If k is a positive
integer such that no k (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in G have pairwise distance in G at least r, then there
exists Z∗⊆V (G) such that Z∗is (ξ∗, η∗)-centered in G and intersects all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in
G.
Proof. Let H be a finite graph, and let k, r ∈N and ℓ∈N0. By Theorem 8.2, there exist
functions ξ, η such that for any finite K|V (H)|+1-minor graph G′, induced subgraph L′ of G′
and X′, Y ′ ⊆V (L′), such that NG′(V (L′)) ⊆Z for some (1, 0)-centered set Z ⊆V (G′) in
G′, if there exist no k (ℓ, X′, Y ′)-paths in (L′, G′) pairwise at distance in G′ at least r, then
there exists a (ξ, η)-centered set in G′ intersecting all (ℓ, X′, Y ′)-paths in (L′, G′). Note that
ξ∗≥ℓ. Define ξ∗= ξ and η∗= 2η.

To prove this lemma, it suffices to show that Z∗intersects all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in G.
Suppose to the contrary that there exists an (ℓ, X, Y )-path P in G disjoint from Z∗. So
c(v) = 2 for every v ∈V (P). Since V (P) ∈A, there exists B ∈A with B ⊇V (P) such
that cB(v) = c(v) = 2 for every v ∈V (P). So P is an (ℓ, X ∩B, Y ∩B)-path in (G[B], G)
disjoint from ZG[B], contradicting Claim 1.
8.3
Apex-minor free graphs
Recall that an apex-graph is a graph that can be made planar by deleting at most one
vertex.
Theorem 3.10 provided a structure theorem for H-minor free graphs for an arbitrary
graph H. If H is an apex-graph, the structure can be further refined to allow us to provide
a much simpler proof for the weak Menger property for H-minor free graphs with a much
better bound. Such a structure theorem is known in the literature, but it was not stated in
the form that is completely ready to apply our machinery. In this section we combine the
known results to derive a form that we will use in later sections.
We show how to slightly revise the proof of [16, Lemma 36] to obtain the following lemma.
Lemma 8.5. For any apex-graph H, surface Σ in which H cannot be drawn and for any
κ, ξ ∈N0 and ρ ∈N, there exist κ∗, ρ∗∈N such that for every θ∗∈N, there exists θ ∈N
such that the following holds. Let G be a graph, and let Z ⊆V (G) with |Z| ≤ξ. Let T be
a tangle in G of order at least θ + |Z|. Let S be an effective (T −Z)-central segregation of
G −Z with a (κ, ρ)-witness (S1, S2) such that there exists a (Σ, θ, θ, T −Z)-arrangement of
S with respect to (S1, S2). If H is not a minor of G, then there exists an effective (T −Z)-
central segregation S∗of G −Z with a (κ∗, ρ∗)-witness (S∗
1, S∗
2) such that there exists a
(Σ, θ∗, 1, T −Z)-arrangement of S∗with respect to (S∗

We will use the following restatement of [16, Theorem 28].
Theorem 8.6 ([16, Theorem 28]). For every graph H, there exist κ, ρ ∈N0 such that for
every nondecreasing function ϕ : Z →R>0, there exist ξ, θ ∈N0 with θ > ξ such that if G
is an H-minor free graph with a tangle T of order at least θ, then there exist Z ⊆V (G)
with |Z| ≤ξ and an effective (T −Z)-central segregation S of G −Z with respect to a
(κ, ρ)-witness (S1, S2) such that S has a (Σ, ϕ(|Z|), ϕ(|Z|), T −Z)-arrangement with respect
to (S1, S2) for some surface Σ in which H cannot be drawn.
Now we prove the structure theorem for apex-minor free graphs that we will use.

Quasi-isometry
We prove Theorem 1.12 in this section.
Recall that we say that an R>0-weighted graph or a length space (X, dX) has the weak
coarse Menger property if there exist functions f, g : N × R>0 →R such that for any
k ∈N, r ∈R>0 and subsets A, B of X, either there exist k paths in (X, dX) from A to B
pairwise at distance in dX at least r, or there exists Z ⊆X such that Z is (f(k, r), g(k, r))-
centered and intersects every path in (X, dX) from A to B. We call (f, g) a witness for the
weak coarse Menger property.
Similarly, we say that an R>0-weighted graph or a length space (X, dX) has the remote
weak coarse Menger property if there exist functions f, g : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R such that for
any subsets A, B of X and k ∈N, r ∈R>0, ℓ∈R≥0, either there exist k (ℓ, A, B)-paths in
(X, dX) from A to B pairwise at distance in dX at least r, or there exists Z ⊆X such that Z
is (f(k, r, ℓ), g(k, r, ℓ))-centered and intersects every (ℓ, A, B)-path in (X, dX). We call (f, g)
a witness for the remote weak coarse Menger property.
Moreover, we say that an R>0-weighted graph or a length space (X, dX) has the weak
coarse Gallai property if there exist functions f, g : N × R>0 →R such that for any k ∈
N, r ∈R≥0 and subset A of X, either there exist k A-paths in (X, dX) pairwise at distance in
dX at least r, or there exists Z ⊆X such that Z is (f(k, r), g(k, r))-centered and intersects
every A-path in (X, dX). We call (f, g) a witness for the weak coarse Gallai property.
We first show that those properties are preserved under quasi-isometry.
Lemma 9.1. For any m ≥1 and a ≥0, there exist c1, c2 > 0 such that the following holds.
Let (X, dX) and (Y, dY ) be finite or infinite (0, 1]-weighted graphs or length spaces. Let ι be
an (m, a)-quasi-isometry from (X, dX) to (Y, dY ) for some m ≥1 and a ≥0.
1. If (Y, dY ) has the weak coarse Menger property with witness (f(k, r), g(k, r)) for some
functions f, g : N × R>0 →R, then (X, dX) has the weak coarse Menger property with
witness (f(k, mr + c1), 2m · g(k, mr + c1) + c2).
92


## Page 93


2. If (Y, dY ) has the remote weak coarse Menger property with witness (f(k, r, ℓ), g(k, r, ℓ))
for some functions f, g : N×R>0 ×R≥0 →R, then (X, dX) has the remote weak coarse
Menger property with witness (f(k, mr + c1, mℓ+ 3a) + k −1, 2m · g(k, mr + c1, mℓ+
3a) + 2m2ℓ+ r + c2).
3. If (Y, dY ) has the weak coarse Gallai property with witness (f(k, r), g(k, r)) for some
functions f, g : N × R>0 →R, then (X, dX) has the weak coarse Gallai property with
witness (f(k, mr + c1), 2m · g(k, mr + c1) + c2).
Proof. We only prove Statement 2, since Statements 1 and 3 are analogous and simpler.
Let f, g : N × R>0 × R≥0. Let k ∈N, and let r > 0 and ℓ≥0 be real numbers. Let
r′ = 2m2(3a + 1) + (2 + r)m + 3a and ℓ′ = mℓ+ 3a. Let ξ1 = f(k, r′, ℓ′) and η1 = g(k, r′, ℓ′).
Let η2 = m(2η1 +3a). Let η3 = η2 +m(m+2a+1). Let ℓ′′ = (ℓ′ +a)m. Let η4 = 2ℓ′′ +2+r.
Define f ′(k, r, ℓ) = ξ1 + k −1 and g′(k, r, ℓ) = max{η3, η4}.
Note that f ′(k, r, ℓ) = f(k, r′, ℓ′) + k −1 and g′(k, r, ℓ) ≤(2g(k, r′, ℓ′) + m + 8a + 1 +
2mℓ)m+r+2. Let c1 = 2m2(3a+1)+2m+3a. So r′ = mr+c1. Let c2 = (m+8a+1)m+2.
Then g′(k, r, ℓ) ≤2m · g(k, r′, ℓ′) + 2m2ℓ+ r + c2.
Let A, B be subsets of X. We assume that there exist no k (ℓ, A, B)-paths in (X, dX) with
pairwise distance in dX at least r. It suffices to show that there exists an (f ′(k, r, ℓ), g′(k, r, ℓ))-
centered set in (X, dX) intersecting all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (X, dX).
Let ι be an (m, a)-quasi-isometry from (X, dX) to (Y, dY ). Then for every y ∈Y , there
exists xy ∈X such that dY (ι(xy), y) ≤a. Note that we may assume that ι(xy) = y and
xy ∈W for any W = {A, B} and y ∈ι(W).
Claim 1: There do not exist k (ℓ′, ι(A), ι(B))-paths in (Y, dY ) with pairwise distance in dY
at least r′.
Proof of Claim 1: Suppose to the contrary that there exist k (ℓ′, ι(A), ι(B))-paths P1, P2, ...,
Pk in (Y, dY ) with pairwise distance in dY at least r′.
For every i ∈[k], let ai and bi be the ends of Pi. Note that for every i ∈[k], there exists
a finite sequence pi,1, pi,2, ..., pi,ci over Pi for some positive integer ci such that pi,1 = ai,
pi,ci = bi and dY (pi,j, pi,j+1) ≤1 for every j ∈[ci −1]; we let V (Pi) = {pi,j : j ∈[ci]}. Since
ai ∈ι(A) and bi ∈ι(B), we have xai ∈A and xbi ∈B. For every i ∈[k], let Wi = {xy ∈X :
y ∈V (Pi)}; note that for any j ∈[ci −1], dX(xpi,j, xpi,j+1) ≤m(dY (ι(xpi,j), ι(xpi,j+1)) + a) ≤
m(dY (pi,j, pi,j+1) + 2a + a) ≤m(3a + 1), and there exists a path Qi,j in (X, dX) from xpi,j to
xpi,j+1 with length in dX at most dX(xpi,j, xpi,j+1)+1 ≤m(3a+1)+1; we let Qi = Sci−1
j=1 Qi,j.
Note that each Qi contains a path in (X, dX) from xai ∈A to xbi ∈B.
Now we show that Q1, Q2, ..., Qk have pairwise distance in dX at least r. Suppose to the
contrary that there exist i1 < i2 such that dX(Qi1, Qi2) < r. Then there exist j1, j2 such that
dX(Qi1,j1, Qi2,j2) < r. So dX(xpi1,j1, xpi2,j2) < 2 · (m(3a + 1) + 1) + r = 2m(3a + 1) + 2 + r.
Hence dY (ι(xpi1,j1), ι(xpi2,j2)) < m·(2m(3a+1)+2+r)+a = 2m2(3a+1)+(2+r)m+a. So
dY (Pi1, Pi2) ≤dY (pi1,j1, pi2,j2) ≤dY (ι(xpi1,j1), ι(xpi2,j2))+2a < 2m2(3a+1)+(2+r)m+3a = r′,
a contradiction.
Hence Q1, Q2, ..., Qk have pairwise at distance in dX at least r. Note that for any i ∈[k],
dX(xai, xbi) ≥1
m · (dY (ι(xai), ι(xbi)) −a) ≥1
m · (dY (ai, bi) −2a −a) ≥1
m · (ℓ′ −3a) ≥ℓ. So for
every i ∈[k], Qi contains an (ℓ, X, Y )-path in (X, dX). Hence there exist k (ℓ, X, Y )-paths
in (X, dX) with pairwise distance in dX at least r, a contradiction. □

Lemma 9.2. For any (finite) graph H, real numbers m, a with m ≥1 and a ≥0, there exist
functions f : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R and g : R>0 × R≥0 →R such that if (W, dW) is a length
space that is (m, a)-quasi-isometric to an H-minor free finite or locally finite infinite graph,
then
1. for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0, ℓ∈R≥0 and subsets X, Y of W, either there exist k (ℓ, X, Y )-
paths in (W, dW) pairwise at distance in dW at least r, or there exists an (f(k, r, ℓ), g(r, ℓ))-
centered set in (W, dW) intersecting all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (W, dW), and
2. for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0 and subset A of W, either there exist k A-paths in (W, dW)
pairwise at distance in dW at least r, or there exists an (f(k, r, 0), g(r, 0))-centered set
in (W, dW) intersecting all A-paths in (W, dW).

Moreover, g is linear in the first variable.
Proof. Since the distance between any two vertices in a finite or locally finite infinite graph
is at least 1, Theorem 8.4 implies that there exist functions f1 : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R and
g1 : R>0 × R≥0 →R such that every H-minor free finite or locally finite infinite graph has
the remote weak coarse Menger property with witness (f1, g1). Note that g1 is linear in the
first variable. By considering (0, A, A)-paths, it implies that every H-minor free finite or
locally finite infinite graph has the remote weak coarse Gallai property with witness (f2, g2)
for some functions f2 : N × R>0 →R and g2 : R>0 →R. Note that g2 is linear. Then this
lemma immediately follows from Lemma 9.1.
Now we extend Lemma 9.2 to length spaces quasi-isometric to locally finite H-minor free
metric graphs. We say that a metric graph is finite if it is defined by a finite R>0-weighted
graph.
Lemma 9.3. For any (finite) graph H, real numbers m, a with m ≥1 and a ≥0, there exist
functions f : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R and g : R>0 × R≥0 →R such that if (W, dW) is a length
space that is (m, a)-quasi-isometric to an H-minor free finite or locally finite infinite metric
graph, then
1. for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0, ℓ∈R≥0 and subsets X, Y of W, either there exist k (ℓ, X, Y )-
paths in (W, dW) pairwise at distance in dW at least r, or there exists an (f(k, r, ℓ), g(r, ℓ))-
centered set in (W, dW) intersecting all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (W, dW), and
2. for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0 and subset A of W, either there exist k A-paths in (W, dW)
pairwise at distance in dW at least r, or there exists an (f(k, r, 0), g(r, 0))-centered set
in (W, dW) intersecting all A-paths in (W, dW).
Moreover, g is linear in the first variable.
Proof. Let H be a graph, and let m, a be real numbers with m ≥1 and a ≥0. Let H′ be
a 3-connected graph such that H ⊆H′. By Theorem 1.11, there exist real numbers m1, a1
with m1 ≥1 and a1 ≥0 such that every H′-minor free locally finite (0, 1]-weighted graph is
(m1, a1)-quasi-isometric to an H′-minor free locally finite graph.
We prove Statement 1 of this lemma; the proof of Statement 2 is analogous, and we omit
it.
Let (W, dW) be a length space that is (m, a)-quasi-isometric to an H-minor free finite
or locally finite infinite metric graph (W0, d0).
Let (W ′, ϕ′) be the R>0-weighted graph
that defines metric graph (W0, d0). Let (W ′′, ϕ′′) be a (0, 1]-weighted graph obtained from
subdividing edges of W ′ such that dist(W ′′,ϕ′′)(u, v) = dist(W ′,ϕ′)(u, v) for any u, v ∈V (W ′).
Since W ′ is H-minor free, W ′ is also H′-minor free. Since H′ is 3-connected, W ′′ is H′-minor
free. By Theorem 1.11, there exists an H′-minor free locally finite graph W ′′′ such that
(W ′′, ϕ′′) is (m1, a1)-quasi-isometric to W ′′′.
Let (W1, d1) be the metric graph defined by the (0, 1]-weighted graph (W ′′, ϕ′′). Then
(W1, d1) is a length space (1, 1)-quasi-isometric to (W ′′, ϕ′′). So W1 is (m1, a1 + 1)-quasi-
isometric to W ′′′.
By Lemma 9.2, there exist functions f1 : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R and
g1 : R>0×R≥0 →R only dependent on H′, m1, a1 (and hence only dependent on H, m, a) such
95

that for any k ∈N, ℓ∈R>0, r ∈R≥0 and subsets X, Y of W1, either there exist k (ℓ, X, Y )-
paths in (W1, d1) pairwise at distance in d1 at least r, or there exists an (f1(k, r, ℓ), g1(r, ℓ))-
centered set in (W1, d1) intersecting all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (W1, d1). Moreover, g1 is linear in
the first variable r.
Since W0 ⊆W1, we know that for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0, ℓ∈R≥0 and subsets X, Y of
V (W0), either there exist k (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (W0, d0) pairwise at distance in d0 at least r,
or there exists an (f1(k, r, ℓ), g1(r, ℓ))-centered set in (W0, d0) intersecting all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths
in (W0, d0). Since (W, dW) is (m, a)-quasi-isometric to the length space (W0, d0), Lemma 9.1
implies that there exist functions f : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R and g : R>0 × R≥0 →R only
dependent on f1, g1, m, a (and hence only dependent on H, m, a) such that this lemma holds.
Lemma 9.3 allows us to extend our results to weighted graphs from length spaces.
Lemma 9.4. For any (finite) graph H, there exist functions f : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R and
g : R>0 × R≥0 →R such that if (G, ϕ) is an H-minor free finite or locally finite infinite
R>0-weighted graph, then
1. for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0, ℓ∈R≥0 and subsets X, Y of W, either there exist k (ℓ, X, Y )-
paths in (G, ϕ) pairwise at distance in (G, ϕ) at least r, or there exists an (f(k, r, ℓ),
g(r, ℓ))-centered set in (W, dW) intersecting all (ℓ, X, Y )-paths in (G, ϕ), and
2. for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0 and subset A of W, either there exist k A-paths in (G, ϕ)
pairwise at distance in (G, ϕ) at least r, or there exists an (f(k, r, 0), g(r, 0))-centered
set in (W, dW) intersecting all A-paths in (G, ϕ).
Moreover, g is linear in the first variable.
Proof.
Let H′ be a 3-connected graph such that H ⊆H′.
Let (G′, ϕ′) be the (0, 1]-
weighted graph obtained from (G, ϕ) by subdividing edges of G such that dist(G′,ϕ′)(u, v) =
dist(G,ϕ)(u, v) for any u, v ∈V (G). Since G is H-minor free, G is H′-minor free, and hence
G′ is H′-minor free. Let (W, dW) be the metric graph defined by (G′, ϕ′). Then (G′, ϕ′)
is (1, 1)-quasi-isometric to (W, dW). So Lemma 9.3 implies that there exist functions f :
N×R>0×R≥0 →R and g : R>0×R≥0 →R only dependent on H′ (and hence only dependent
on H) such that the conclusions of this lemma hold for (G′, ϕ′). Since V (G) ⊆V (G′), this
lemma holds for (G, ϕ).
Now we introduce a trick that allows us to remove the dependency of r from the function
f in the witness for all our previous theorems. In order to do so, we consider our properties
for classes of length spaces or weighted graphs. We say that a class F of length spaces
or R>0-weighted graphs has the weak coarse Menger property (and the remote weak coarse
Menger property and weak coarse Gallai property, respectively) if there exist functions f, g
such that every member of F has the weak coarse Menger property (and the remote weak
coarse Menger property and weak coarse Gallai property, respectively) with witness (f, g).
We call (f, g) the witness for F.
A set F of metric spaces is scaling-closed if (X, r · dX) ∈F for any (X, dX) ∈F and real
number r > 0.
96


## Page 97


Lemma 9.5. Let F be a scaling-closed set of length spaces or R>0-weighted graphs.
1. If f, g : N × R →R are functions such that every member of F has the weak coarse
Menger property with witness (f(k, r), g(k, r)), then every length space in F has the
weak coarse Menger property with witness (f(k, 1), g(k, 1) · r).
2. If f, g : N × R →R are functions such that every member of F has the weak coarse
Gallai property with witness (f(k, r), g(k, r)), then every length space in F has the weak
coarse Gallai property with witness (f(k, 1), g(k, 1) · r).
3. If f, g : N×R>0 ×R≥0 →R are functions such that every member of F has the remote
weak coarse Menger property with witness (f(k, r, ℓ), g(k, r, ℓ)), then every length space
in F has the remote weak coarse Menger property with witness (f(k, 1, ℓ
r), g(k, 1, ℓ
r)·r).
Proof.
We only prove Statement 3 because the proofs of the first two statements are
analogous and simpler.
Let (X, dX) ∈F, and let k ∈N, r > 0 and ℓ≥0. Let A, B ⊆X. Since F is scaling-closed,
(X, 1
r · dX) ∈F. Note that every (ℓ, A, B)-path in (X, dX) is an ( ℓ
r, A, B)-path in (X, 1
r · dX),
and vice versa. If there exist k ( ℓ
r, A, B)-paths in (X, 1
r ·dX) pairwise at distance in (X, 1
r ·dX)
at least 1, then there exist k (ℓ, A, B)-paths in (X, dX) pairwise at distance in (X, dX) at
least r. Otherwise, since (X, 1
r · dX) ∈F, there exists a (f(k, 1, ℓ
r), g(k, 1, ℓ
r))-centered set Z
in (X, 1
r · dX) intersecting all ( ℓ
r, A, B)-paths in (X, 1
r · dX), so Z is a (f(k, 1, ℓ
r), g(k, 1, ℓ
r) · r)-
centered set in (X, dX) intersecting all (ℓ, A, B)-paths in (X, dX). Therefore, (X, dX) has the
remote coarse Menger property with the desired witness.
Now we prove a special case of Theorem 1.12 for which the quasi-isometry has zero
additive distortion.
Lemma 9.6. Let H be a graph, and let m ≥1 be a real number. Let F1 be the set of finite
or locally finite infinite H-minor free R>0-weighted graphs. Let F2 be the set of finite or
locally finite infinite H-minor free metric graphs. Let F3 be the set of length spaces that are
(m, 0)-quasi-isometric to a member of F2. Let F = F1 ∪F2 ∪F3. Then the following hold:
1. There exist a function f : N →R and a real number c such that every member of F
has the weak coarse Menger property with witness (f, cx).
2. There exist a function f : N →R and a real number c such that every member of F
has the weak coarse Gallai property with witness (f, cx).
3. For every α ≥0, there exist a function f : N →R and a real number c such that if
(W, dW) ∈F, then for any k ∈N, real number r > 0 and subsets X and Y of W,
either there exist k (αr, X, Y )-paths in (W, dW) pairwise at distance in dW at least r,
or there exists an (f(k), cr)-centered set intersecting all (αr, X, Y )-paths in (W, dW).
Proof. Let H, m, F1, F2, F3, F be as stated in the lemma. Obviously, F1 ∪F2 is scaling-
closed. This implies that F3 is scaling-closed. So F is scaling-closed.
Lemmas 9.3 and 9.4 imply that there exist functions f1 : N × R>0 × R≥0 →R, g1 :
R>0 × R≥0 →R, f2 : N × R>0 →R and g2 : R>0 →R such that F satisfies the remote
97


## Page 98


weak coarse Menger property with witness (f1(k, r, ℓ), g1(r, ℓ)) and satisfies the weak coarse
Gallai property with witness (f2(k, r), g2(r)), and g1, g2 are linear in their first variable.
Taking ℓ= 0, we know that F satisfies the weak coarse Menger property with witness
(f1(k, r, 0), g1(r, 0)). Note that f1, g1, f2, g2 only depend on H.
Since F is scaling-closed, Lemma 9.5 implies that
• F has the weak coarse Menger property with (f1(k, 1, 0), g1(1, 0) · r),
• F has the weak coarse Gallai property with (f2(k, 1), g2(1) · r), and
• F has the remote weak coarse Menger property with witness (f1(k, 1, ℓ
r), g1(1, ℓ
r) · r).
Statements 1 and 2 follows from the first two bullets, respectively. For every α ≥0, let
f : N →R be the function such that f(x) = f1(x, 1, α) for every x ∈N, and let c = g1(1, α).
Then Statement 3 of this lemma holds.
Now we are ready to prove Theorem 1.12. The following is an equivalent statement.
Theorem 9.7. Let H be a (finite) graph. Let m ≥1, a ≥0 be real numbers. Let F1 be the
set of finite or locally finite infinite H-minor free R>0-weighted graphs. Let F2 be the set of
finite or locally finite infinite H-minor free metric graphs. Let F3 be the set of length spaces
that are (m, a)-quasi-isometric to a member of F2. Let F4 be the set of length spaces that are
(m, a)-quasi-isometric to a (0, 1]-weighted graph in F1. Let F = F1 ∪F2 ∪F3 ∪F4. Then
the following hold:
1. There exist a function f : N →R and real numbers c, c′ ≥0 such that if (W, dW) ∈F,
then for any k ∈N, real number r > 0 and subsets X and Y of W, either there exist
k (X, Y )-paths in (W, dW) pairwise at distance in dW at least r, or there exists an
(f(k), cr + c′)-centered set intersecting all (X, Y )-paths in (W, dW).
2. There exist a function f : N →R and real numbers c, c′ ≥0 such that if (W, dW) ∈F,
then for any k ∈N, real number r > 0 and subset A of W, either there exist k A-paths
in (W, dW) pairwise at distance in dW at least r, or there exists an (f(k), cr + c′)-
centered set intersecting all A-paths in (W, dW).
3. For every α ≥0, there exist a function f : N →R and real numbers c, c′ ≥0 such
that if a = 0 and (W, dW) ∈F1 ∪F2 ∪F3, then for any k ∈N, real number r > 0
and subsets X and Y of W, either there exist k (αr, X, Y )-paths in (W, dW) pairwise
at distance in dW at least r, or there exists an (f(k), cr + c′)-centered set intersecting
all (αr, X, Y )-paths in (W, dW).
Moreover, if a = 0 and (W, dW) ∈F1 ∪F2 ∪F3, then c′ can be chosen to be 0.
Proof.
Let H, m, a, F1, F2, F3, F4, F be as stated in the theorem.
The case (W, dW) ∈
F1 ∪F2 and the case a = 0 with (W, dW) ∈F1 ∪F2 ∪F3 have been proved in Lemma
9.6. So we may assume a > 0, and it suffices to prove Statements 1 and 2 for members
(W, dW) ∈F −(F1 ∪F2). Let (W, dW) ∈F −(F1 ∪F2) ⊆F3 ∪F4. So (W, dW) is a length
space (m, a)-quasi-isometric to a member of F2 or a (0, 1]-weighted graph in F1. Hence
Statements 1 and 2 for (W, dW) follow from Lemma 9.1 and Statements 1 and 2 of Lemma
9.6.

## Page 99


10
Concluding remarks
A special case of the main result of this paper states that for any graph H and real
numbers m ≥1, a ≥0, there exist a function f : N →R and a real number c > 0 such
that if G is a graph that is (m, a)-quasi-isometric to an H-minor free graph, then for any
k ∈N, r ∈R>0 and X, Y ⊆V (G), either there exist k paths from X to Y pairwise at
distance at least r, or there exists an (f(k), cr)-centered set intersecting all paths in G from
X to Y . Like asymptotic dimension of metric spaces can be used to distinguish metric spaces
up to quasi-isometry, so does this result. Georgakopoulos and Papasoglu [21] conjectured
the following coarse analog of Wagner’s characterization for planar graphs:
Conjecture 10.1 ([21, Conjecture 9.1]). A length space is quasi-isometric to a planar graph
if and only if it has no asymptotic K5 or K3,3 minor.
We refer interested readers to [21] for the definition of asymptotic minors of length spaces.
By our aforementioned result, a negative answer to the following question would disprove
Conjecture 10.1.
Question 10.2. For every ℓ∈N, do there exist a function f : N →R and a real number
c > 0 such that if G does not contain an ℓ-fat K5-minor model nor an ℓ-fat K3,3-minor
model, then for any k ∈N, r ∈R>0 and X, Y ⊆V (G), either there exist k paths from X
to Y pairwise at distance at least r, or there exists an (f(k), cr)-centered set intersecting all
paths in G from X to Y ?
Many problems in combinatorial optimization can be stated as a packing problem or a
covering problem. The packing problem and the covering problem can be formulated as
integer programming problems, and they are dual to each other. Menger’s theorem states
that the optimal value for packing X-Y paths equals the optimal value for covering X-Y
paths. In general, the optimal values for packing and covering are not necessarily related,
except for the weak duality that the optimal value for covering is at least the optimal value
for packing. Erd˝os-P´osa property captures the case that the optimal values of the integer
programming problems for packing and for covering are tied to each other.
Paths between two prescribed sets X and Y are exactly rooted subdivisions of K2. It is
well known that the set of rooted subdivisions of P3 does not have the Erd˝os-P´osa property.
On the other hand, Liu [32] proved that for every graph H, the set of rooted subdivisions of
H has the half-integral Erd˝os-P´osa property. Roughly speaking, the half-integral Erd˝os-P´osa
property captures the case that the optimal values for packing and for covering are tied to
each other if we allow the solution for the packing problem to be an half-integral vector.
We propose the following conjecture, which is the half-integral weakening of Conjecture
1.2.
Conjecture 10.3. There exist functions f, g : N × R>0 →R such that for any k ∈N, r ∈
R>0, graph G and subsets X, Y of V (G), either
1. there exist k X-Y paths P1, P2, ..., Pk in G such that every ball of radius r in G intersects
at most two of P1, P2, ..., Pk, or
2. there exists an (f(k, r), g(k, r))-centered set intersecting all X-Y paths in G.
